### Current Admission — Approved Pickup Mix-up Walkthrough

2026-10-31: Ivan explicitly approves the eight-step story below and requests implementation. Same PORT-17, LOCAL3140 only; accountable PRODUCT, current executor FRONTEND → existing QA → PRODUCT/Ivan. This supersedes the older provisional Pickup main-story sequence, not accepted Lost/map/avatar behavior. No Figma redesign or further design approval required.

Visible gap: Pickup has prepared fixtures, map, generic replies and promo controls but lacks the approved causal walkthrough. Canonical owner is the existing scenario reducer, derived checklist/suggestions and shared preview/shell seams. Reuse direction1: existing Lost staged-delivery/run guards, immediate-focus suggestions, three tones, native promo card, recipient controls, retained Order/Track, DemoMotion and completion cover. Extend the existing scenario owner; no second workflow engine, duplicated shell, new service or generalized framework.

| Step | Operator / UI | Scripted message / next state |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | Select Pickup mix-up; current trip already selected, Order visible | Jason: “Hi, I’m at the main entrance, but I can’t see my driver. Could you help?” |
| 2 | Cmd/Ctrl+A → Open Trip Track; inspect prepared timestamped rider/main and driver/north positions; close to chat | Two fictional meeting points near Praça da Alegria, not verified real entrances or proof of driver fault |
| 3 | Focus rider composer, suggestion visible immediately; Tab accepts, Send | Operator: “Thanks for waiting, Jason. Ivan is at the north entrance. Please stay at the main entrance; I’ll ask him to meet you there.” |
| 4 | Existing typing delay, then rider response | Jason: “I selected the main entrance when I booked. I’ve been waiting here the whole time.” |
| 5 | Switch composer to Ivan · Driver; retained Order stays visible; send prepared reply | Operator: “Hi Ivan, Jason is waiting at the main entrance on Praça da Alegria. Please meet him there and confirm.” |
| 6 | Existing typing delay, then driver response | Ivan: “Understood. I’m heading to the main entrance now. I’ll be there shortly.” |
| 7 | Switch back to Jason · Passenger; deliberately attach native €5 promo card via attachment menu and send together | Operator: “I’m sorry about the confusion and the wait, Jason. Ivan is coming to the main entrance. Here’s €5 off your next trip.” |
| 8 | Existing typing delay, then rider confirmation; Complete opens existing portfolio cover | Jason: “Thanks, I’ve received the code. I can see Ivan now!” |

Guards: no acknowledgement before its triggering message/recipient/stage; no “code received” without sent €5 promo attachment; attach/remove/cancel or wrong-recipient/arbitrary Send cannot skip stages. Promo is fictional goodwill for the next trip, not payment mutation, real entitlement or historical policy. Three Super Warm/Neutral/Strict variants preserve the same facts and transitions. Composer remains focusable at all stages with existing action guidance; blocked drafts cannot advance story. Map inspection never confirms a trip or advances story by itself; reopening remains available anytime and retains current trip/context. Keep current order/recipient/call layout and all map source/provider/colors/road geometry. Do not invent live movement or driver-fault proof. Complete only after the causal promo reply and rider meeting confirmation. Restart/new-scenario resets pending reply/stage/draft/attachments safely while preserving theme; repeated active tab does not reset unexpectedly.

Implementation order / acceptance: (1) inspect existing owners and add the smallest Pickup stages/fixtures/suggestions/checklist, (2) wire current controls and shared completion without changing Lost/Income contracts, (3) focused reducer/three-tone/promo/recipient/pending/reset regressions plus current Citymobil suite, affected types and hygiene/diff, (4) native full Pickup walkthrough through map, recipient switch, attached promo and Complete; wrong recipient/missing or removed promo/duplicate Send/reset-while-typing checks; retained Order/Track and immediate-focus Tab behavior, keyboard/focus, desktop/narrow Light/Dark and overflow/errors; one Lost completion regression and Income smoke, (5) source/evidence/cleanup/readback then direct existing QA for the changed causal boundary. QA reuses unchanged map road/color/wheel and prior full-site evidence, validates new flow and Lost regression, and returns defects directly to FRONTEND. No whole-site build unless actual affected closure requires it. Task stays open for skipped stages, stale/duplicate replies, false promo success, inaccessible completion/focus, Lost regression or missing native proof.

Preserve original assets/identities, accepted latest-Track selection versus Lost confirmation boundary and avatar crop;3141, typography choice, navigation/native-hidden deferrals and prior diagnostic/hardware limits stay untouched. No packages/provider, actual call/payment/coupon/AI/backend/persistence, source-Figma, public-site/Git/release or Income walkthrough scope. Record the exact delta and compact proof here; no new Task or prompt chain.

Ivan reports a fresh LOCAL Lost-item failure: an Order with a real prepared historical trip is visible, but Open Trip Track shows "Select a trip to view its historical track"; clicking another trip does not reliably open/update the map, and Order can be clipped when the map is open. The reducer deliberately guards story-confirmed selection until the rider has confirmed the trip; `CitymobilTrack` needs an explicit Lost trip ID to resolve prepared geography. Open Trip Track from the left action, Cmd/Ctrl+A menu, existing direct map shortcut and Order's Show On Map must consistently open the prepared track for the explicit/currently viewed Order trip; a confirmed trip is the fallback only when there is no viewed context. If no valid trip is available, provide an actionable trip-selection state, not a misleading route with an inert "select" paragraph. No invented prepared data for unsupported cases.

Closing/reopening Track, switching recipient, opening calls or changing theme must not silently lose the selected viewing context or close Order; explicit Order close remains separate. Closing Order must not invalidate a still-valid displayed historical track.

All Order header actions/tabs/route/map button/Close remain reachable, with readable map controls/attribution and no off-viewport panel edge or unintended horizontal page overflow. ## Hiring Reading Hierarchy

The opening must answer, without scrolling through a process essay:

- What is the product and whom does it serve?
- What problem was Ivan designing for?
- What was his own role/contribution?
- Which company/work context does the project belong to?
- What can the visitor inspect as evidence?

Use a concise non-metric summary and role metadata beside a strong product visual. Treat a 30–60-second recruiter scan as a design goal, not a measured usability result. Deeper reading should explain decisions rather than show an uncaptioned screenshot dump.

## Hero Alternatives

Keep typography, palette, source copy and navigation consistent so the alternatives compare composition, not three unrelated brands.

1. **Split editorial — recommended starting point.** Narrow left title/summary/attribution/role; larger right actual workspace evidence. Text is sticky only within the section. Prefer readable UI over a tiny laptop mockup.
2. **Wide product first.** Compact introductory text followed by a nearly full-width product view. Keep essential role/context visible before or beside the visual; do not make HR decipher the product from imagery alone.
3. **Motion-led split.** Short left overview with a right video-poster/still and explicit play affordance; a large detail can continue below. Motion must not be the only way to understand the case.

Show all three hero alternatives, then extend one recommended composition into the full page. These are proposals for review, not three approved implementation variants. The Designer may refine the precise grid after seeing the assets and should explain the recommendation.

## Proposed Page Architecture

1. **Contextual header.** Back outside/at the edge of the content lane, concise Citymobil project context, existing contacts. Reuse the latest minimal runtime header intent: single-line compact height 44px, aligned text baselines and 44px interaction targets. Preserve mobile Contact sheet intent. The older Figma header study is not authority to restore extra height.
2. **Hero / project overview.** Product name, clear support-workspace summary, company/work-context link, project role and evidence visual. Separate employment dates from missing project dates; avoid a duplicate decorative title wall.
3. **Problem and contribution.** Short explanation of fragmented support context and what Ivan designed. No fabricated research quotations, team composition, release status or numeric baselines.
4. **One workspace.** Large overall view with a short narrative explaining the relationship between conversation, trip information and actions. A clear full product view should precede tight crops.
5. **Context at the point of action.** Sticky text left / trip-context media right. A video or sequence may show how the operator accesses relevant information.
6. **Guided resolution.** Reverse the visual/text balance for scripts and suggested actions; optional concise handoff example. Each subsection has one takeaway and a caption explaining what the evidence demonstrates.
7. **Interaction spotlight.** One short original recording or annotated sequence, potentially full-width. Distinguish original recording, later reconstruction and potential clickable demo. A clickable demo is optional design intent, not new implementation or proof of original production behavior.
8. **What the work demonstrates / reflection.** Source-grounded design contribution and explicit evidence limits. Do not turn intended benefits into measured shipped outcomes. Use verified business results only if separately sourced.
9. **Continuation.** Next project / all projects and contact, using existing link primitives. No visitor chat.

This ordering is a product starting point; DESIGNER can combine redundant chapters to keep the story short and explain any change. Do not impose a generic double-diamond story unsupported by the materials.

## Reusable Section Grammar And Responsive Intent

Use a small set of case-page modules rather than one-off compositions for every screenshot:

| Pattern | Purpose | Desktop intent | Mobile intent |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Narrative + evidence | Explain a decision while inspecting UI | Narrow sticky text, wider media | Text then media; no sticky narrative |
| Evidence + note | Change pacing / spotlight one detail | Large left visual, short right note | Preserve logical reading order; caption stays with asset |
| Wide evidence | Show overall architecture or a rich scene | Shared wider case grid, optional full-bleed | Readable crop/preview plus full-image access intent |
| Video / sequence | Demonstrate a specific interaction | Poster, explicit controls, caption | Tap controls at least 44px; static alternative |
| Outcome / continuation | Close the story and allow onward action | Short text and existing links | Natural wrap, no competing full-screen CTA |

- Allow a wider case grid than the homepage's 800px prose lane; align it consistently with the existing header/content system. Do not change the homepage grid as part of this Task.
- Sticky narratives clear the compact header, release at their own section boundary and fall back to normal flow when too tall for the viewport. No scrolljacking, forced panels or viewport-height text clipping.
- Media retains aspect ratio; no invented/recolored UI. A full-width block is not automatically full viewport height. Avoid stretching small crops to hero size.
- Describe full-image/video viewer entry/exit, keyboard Close/Escape, focus restoration and browser Back intent. Do not certify runtime behavior from a Figma annotation.
- Retain the existing repeated fade-in/out direction as future implementation intent. Keep text readable at rest; no initial blank-content dependency, mandatory motion or autoplay audio. Include reduced-motion and static poster behavior.

Do not import an external system, create a parallel collection, publish libraries or change Foundations.

Four scene types appear in one story: viewport photograph/compact overview; problem and contribution with text left/media right; trip context with the same module reversed; guided resolution with a large full-envelope image and caption. The next-action recording still reuses the side-by-side pattern, followed by compact reflection and existing destinations. No additional comparison/detail family was added. The current sources already explain these decisions without an invented before/after claim.

Design-system decision: reuse existing portfolio primitives and extend their existing seam. `Case / Narrative` `2516:2`, the three existing Evidence figure aspect owners, compact Header `2516:77`, local links/arrows/contact instances and the protected overview remain linked. The Narrative owner now uses the existing 8px gap token; redundant eyebrows are hidden in current consumers. Existing main text styles remain SF Pro14/20, with primary/secondary tones from `VariableID:2459:8` / `2459:9`; justified source/provenance captions use12/16. No new variable collection, text scale, external styling family or whole-case clone was created. The subscribed Hito search returned an unrelated block heading and magnify asset; neither replaced the applicable local portfolio owners.

Desktop wide modules use native GRID: 12 equal FLEX columns, 32px gutter, 1280px envelope at x80; spans3/9 yield296/952px and reversed9/3 yields952/296px. The large-image section spans12; its preceding text uses the existing800px reading limit. Mobile uses two equal columns/gutter24 within342px at x24; narrative and media each span2 in that reading order. Grid gap/max/insets resolve existing tokens; integer counts/spans follow the approved PORT-19 recipe, whose API count/span fields are not live variable-bindable. The shown frame geometries are the 1440px and390px design states; responsive runtime behavior has not been built or tested.

Hero motion maps normal scroll progress to movement of the entire document strip. Desktop overview opacity goes1→0 over scroll0→585px; mobile over0→548.6px, equivalent to65% of hero height. The photo moves upward with that same strip and has no independent scale or parallax drift. Reverse progress restores the overview. Resting source frames keep opacity1. The desktop demonstration continues through the first section: narrative top68px = shared header44 + gap24, held from scroll900→1453.33px, then released at the module end. The reversed module uses the same68px offset and begins holding at scroll1713.33px. This is a bounded illustration of the specified scroll mapping, not time-driven behavior intended for the website.

Reduced-motion/static intent: disable scroll-linked opacity and additional sticky translations; keep the overview opaque and all content in normal flow. Mobile has no sticky narrative. An implementation must also fall back to ordinary flow if narrative height cannot fit below the header. The main long-page frames are readable static content; no wheel takeover, snap lock, carousel or hidden initial copy is required. Existing44px header/background were reparented into each case root as its two native fixed children.

All UI evidence remains uncropped with FIT and source aspect preserved: workspace952×605.33 desktop /342×217.46 mobile; info952² /342²; scripts1280² /342²; actions600×800 /342×456. ### Story, Reuse And Interaction Decisions

The complete case follows overview → problem/contribution → wide workspace → context beside conversation → reversed scripts section → two recording keyframes → reflection/evidence boundary → continuation/contact. Each visual has one narrative purpose. A separate handoff chapter was unnecessary; scripts already explains the connected resolution steps. All five admitted section patterns are represented.

The shared case lane is 1280px at 1440 desktop width, 342px at 390 mobile width. The contextual header is one 44px row. Desktop sticky narrative intent starts at 68px (44px header + 24px gap), releases at its own section boundary and falls back to normal flow if taller than the viewport. Mobile uses normal flow. Full-case fixed/sticky scrolling is specified, not encoded or exercised by the static composition.

Reused existing Portfolio typography/semantic colors/spacing, plain/internal/external link components `2459:162`, `2459:174`, `2459:186`, left arrow `2459:56` and shared desktop contacts `2487:306`. Existing SF Pro Regular/Medium/Semibold remains bound to the current text roles. No variable/style collection or external visual system was added; the file still has 136 Portfolio variables and 19 Portfolio text styles. Selected shared definitions read back unchanged.

Repeated proposal-owned modules live on the new page: Narrative `2516:2`, Overview `2516:15`, compact contextual-header set `2516:77` (desktop `2516:33`, mobile `2516:66`), narrative/evidence set `2516:117` (left `2516:78`, reversed `2516:91`, mobile `2516:104`), and evidence figures wide `2516:6`, square `2519:418`, portrait `2519:423`. Source-owned aspect ratios avoid ineffective instance-only resize overrides. Surrounding portfolio text, links and layout remain editable; only original product UI is raster evidence. These local case modules are proposals, not a published cross-site library.

Original-resolution viewers: desktop workspace/info/scripts `2520:416` / `2520:426` / `2520:436`; mobile `2520:446` / `2520:456` / `2520:466`. Each has a bounded pan viewport and visible 44px Close control. Recording keyframes are `2520:476` desktop and `2520:491` mobile; Contact sheet is `2520:508`. Entry controls have valid native click destinations and minimum 44px targets. Close returns through native BACK. Next/all-project links remain route intent; Email uses the existing destination.

Keyboard Escape, focus containment/restoration, browser Back, reduced-motion behavior and real controlled video playback remain explicit implementation requirements. Text must be readable at rest; repeated fade direction is optional progressive motion with a static alternative and no autoplay audio. Native Figma sequence navigation is not working-video evidence.
